{"parentUuid":"f777e1b0-0c52-45d1-95bf-9cdfced03b74","isSidechain":false,"attachment":{"type":"hook_success","hookName":"PreToolUse:Bash","toolUseID":"toolu_01Wu8YRE8nkGbczLk5vUUb7b","hookEvent":"PreToolUse","content":"","stdout":"{\"hookSpecificOutput\":{\"hookEventName\":\"PreToolUse\",\"permissionDecisionReason\":\"RTK auto-rewrite\",\"updatedInput\":{\"command\":\"rtk gh issue view 1 --comments; rtk read docs/adr/*.md app/main.py app/cli.py app/publish.py tests/conftest.py tests/test_t00.py scripts/seed_demo.py pyproject.toml; gh label list\",\"description\":\"Read spec issue, ADRs, app code, tests, labels\"}}}\n","stderr":"","exitCode":0,"command":"rtk hook claude","durationMs":19},"type":"attachment","uuid":"3ed4c34d-0c60-4967-8bc2-2a49459f4ace","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:46:13.022Z","session_id":"9dbdcd61-9843-4f89-8b21-3507a429536c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2","version":"2.1.309","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"04871c44-bd8f-433a-a758-7f6702183071","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"5be03593-c62d-4407-9739-50e194039a0b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Wu8YRE8nkGbczLk5vUUb7b","type":"tool_result","content":"# ADR-001: The admin console runs locally; the public site is a static export\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nA CMS needs a server, a database, and login. GitHub Pages serves static files\nonly: it cannot run Python and has no database. Students have two weeks and no\nhosting budget, and a first web application with real authentication is an\nattractive target the moment it is public.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe admin console (FastAPI + SQLite) runs on the student's own machine. A\n`cms publish` step renders the published content to static HTML in `site/`, which\nis deployed to GitHub Pages on the `gh-pages` branch.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Login, roles, and the database are never exposed to the internet.\n- The public site cannot have server-side features (search, comments) without a\n  different host; a stretch goal may add a live admin deploy behind a security gate.\n- Publishing is explicit: content changes are not live until `cms publish` runs,\n  which is a behaviour worth documenting for your client.\n- Relative paths become mandatory, because Pages serves the site from a subfolder.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (it shapes every route, template, and deploy step) and it\nis surprising without context (most CMSes are servers). That is the bar; most\ndecisions do not meet it and belong in the spec or the glossary instead.\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. Options\nconsidered: Django (batteries included, but a large amount of generated code to\nread), Flask (similar to FastAPI here), a JavaScript stack (a second language and\na build step), and FastAPI with server-rendered templates.\n\n## Decision\n\nFastAPI with Jinja2 templates, SQLite for storage, argon2 for password hashing,\nand an HTML sanitizer for user Markdown. No frontend framework, no build step.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Every page is server-rendered, so \"view source\" shows what the code produced,\n  which makes debugging visible.\n- SQLite is a single file, so a grader can seed and inspect it easily. It is\n  gitignored; `scripts/seed_demo.py` rebuilds it.\n- No build step means the file you preview is the file that ships.\n- Interactive flourishes (live preview without reload, drag-and-drop) cost more\n  here than in a JavaScript stack. That is an accepted trade.\n# ADR-003: Editors write at the Sunday meetings; only Admins Publish\n\n**Status:** accepted\n**Date:** 2026-10-24\n\n## Context\n\nThe assignment requires two roles, Admin and Editor, with different\npermissions. ADR-001 keeps the admin console on one local machine, the\nPresident's laptop, which never goes on the internet. So an Editor account is\nonly useful if members can get to that laptop. The officers also want to\nreview and edit every Recap before readers see it.\n\n## Decision\n\n- **Editors exist.** Members the President and VP choose get an Editor\n  account, created by an Admin. An Editor writes and edits their own Recaps\n  as Drafts and adds Images with alt text. They cannot Publish or Unpublish,\n  create Trips, manage accounts, Deploy, make Backups, or edit another\n  person's Recap.\n- **Publish is Admin-only.** Admins (President and VP) can do everything an\n  Editor can, plus all of the above, and can edit any Recap.\n- **Editors write at the Sunday club meetings.** The President brings the\n  Console laptop, so members don't have to track an officer down across\n  campus. A member who misses a meeting can email their writing to an Admin,\n  who enters it for them as a Contributor.\n\n## Why Publish stays Admin-only\n\nNothing is public until an Admin Deploys, so letting Editors Publish would\nnot make anything go live sooner. It would only blur which Recaps an Admin has\nactually checked. With Publish kept for Admins, Published means exactly one\nthing: an Admin approved this Recap.\n\n## Considered and rejected\n\n- **No Editor accounts, with Admins entering everything.** Simpler, but it\n  fails the assignment's two-role requirement.\n- **Remote editing, so Editors log in from their own laptops.** This needs the\n  console hosted on the internet, with the security work ADR-001 avoids. Out\n  of scope.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- This departs from the brief's \"editors can publish their own posts\". The\n  Editor role here matches WordPress's Contributor role more than its Editor\n  role.\n- Writing happens on a weekly rhythm. A Recap written between meetings waits\n  for Sunday, or goes through an Admin.\n\"\"\"The FastAPI application.\n\nT00 (already done): the admin console answers at /admin and the public site\nanswers at /. That is the whole skeleton — it exists so you can prove the stack\nruns before you build anything on it.\n\nAdd your routes in their own modules (app/routes/posts.py and so on) and include\nthem here. Keep this file small.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, Request\nfrom fastapi.templating import Jinja2Templates\n\nfrom app import settings\n\ntemplates = Jinja2Templates(directory=str(settings.TEMPLATES))\n\n\ndef create_app() -> FastAPI:\n    app = FastAPI(title=\"IPHS 400 MP2 CMS\")\n\n    @app.get(\"/admin\")\n    def admin_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/hello.html\", {\"title\": \"Admin\"}\n        )\n\n    @app.get(\"/\")\n    def public_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"public/home.html\",\n            {\"title\": settings.SITE_TITLE, \"items\": []},\n        )\n\n    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.\n    #   from app.routes import posts\n    #   app.include_router(posts.router)\n    return app\n\n\napp = create_app()\n\"\"\"The `cms` command: serve, publish, deploy.\n\n    uv run cms serve      # admin console + public preview at http://localhost:8000\n    uv run cms publish    # render site/ from published content\n    uv run cms deploy     # push site/ to the gh-pages branch (Pages serves it)\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfrom app import settings\nfrom app.publish import render_site\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"cms\")\n    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"command\", required=True)\n    serve = sub.add_parser(\"serve\", help=\"run the admin console locally\")\n    serve.add_argument(\"--port\", type=int, default=8000)\n    serve.add_argument(\"--reload\", action=\"store_true\", default=True)\n    sub.add_parser(\"publish\", help=\"render site/ from published content\")\n    deploy = sub.add_parser(\"deploy\", help=\"push site/ to gh-pages\")\n    deploy.add_argument(\"--message\", default=\"Publish site\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    if args.command == \"serve\":\n        import uvicorn\n\n        uvicorn.run(\"app.main:app\", port=args.port, reload=args.reload)\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"publish\":\n        out = render_site()\n        print(f\"Wrote {out}. Preview it with:  python3 -m http.server -d {out} 8001\")\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"deploy\":\n        if not settings.SITE.exists():\n            print(\"site/ does not exist yet — run `uv run cms publish` first.\")\n            return 1\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [sys.executable, \"-m\", \"ghp_import\", \"-n\", \"-p\", \"-m\", args.message,\n             str(settings.SITE)],\n        )\n        if result.returncode == 0:\n            print(\"Pushed to gh-pages. Settings -> Pages -> Deploy from a branch -> \"\n                  \"gh-pages / root, then wait up to 10 minutes.\")\n        return result.returncode\n    return 1\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n\nT00 publishes a placeholder home page. As you build content types, extend\nrender_site() to write one file per published item. Two rules the rubric checks:\n\n  1. Only PUBLISHED content is written here. A draft that reaches site/ is a bug.\n  2. Every href and src is RELATIVE (\"style.css\", \"posts/x.html\"), never\n     root-absolute (\"/style.css\"), because Pages serves this from a subfolder.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader, select_autoescape\n\nfrom app import settings\n\nCSS = \"\"\"/* Minimal starter styles — make them yours. */\n:root { color-scheme: light dark; }\nbody { font: 16px/1.6 system-ui, sans-serif; margin: 0 auto; max-width: 42rem; padding: 1rem; }\nheader a { font-weight: 700; text-decoration: none; }\nmain { margin-block: 2rem; }\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef environment() -> Environment:\n    return Environment(\n        loader=FileSystemLoader(str(settings.TEMPLATES)),\n        autoescape=select_autoescape([\"html\"]),\n    )\n\n\ndef render_site(out: Path | None = None) -> Path:\n    out = out or settings.SITE\n    if out.exists():\n        shutil.rmtree(out)\n    out.mkdir(parents=True)\n    env = environment()\n    (out / \"style.css\").write_text(CSS)\n    (out / \"index.html\").write_text(\n        env.get_template(\"public/home.html\").render(\n            title=settings.SITE_TITLE, items=[], css_path=\"style.css\",\n            home_path=\"index.html\",\n        )\n    )\n    return out\n\"\"\"Shared test fixtures.\n\n`client` gives you the app. `client_as(role)` gives you a client that is logged\nin as a seeded user of that role — it works as soon as your login route exists,\nso access-control tests stay one line:\n\n    def test_editor_cannot_manage_users(client_as):\n        assert client_as(\"editor\").get(\"/admin/users\").status_code in (302, 403)\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\n\nfrom app.main import create_app\n\n# Matches scripts/seed_demo.py. Passwords come from the environment there; in\n# tests they are fixed and meaningless.\nDEMO_USERS = {\n    \"admin\": {\"email\": \"admin@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-admin-pw\"},\n    \"editor\": {\"email\": \"editor@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-editor-pw\"},\n}\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client() -> TestClient:\n    return TestClient(create_app())\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client_as():\n    \"\"\"Return a factory: client_as(\"editor\") -> a logged-in TestClient.\"\"\"\n\n    def _login(role: str) -> TestClient:\n        user = DEMO_USERS[role]\n        c = TestClient(create_app())\n        response = c.post(\"/login\", data={\"email\": user[\"email\"],\n                                          \"password\": user[\"password\"]},\n                          follow_redirects=False)\n        if response.status_code == 404:\n            pytest.skip(\"No /login route yet — build the login ticket first.\")\n        assert response.status_code in (200, 302, 303), (\n            f\"Login as {role} failed with {response.status_code}\")\n        return c\n\n    return _login\n\"\"\"T00: the walking skeleton. These pass in the fresh template.\n\nDo not delete them. If a change breaks them, the change broke the app's front\ndoor.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef test_admin_console_answers(client):\n    response = client.get(\"/admin\")\n    assert response.status_code == 200\n    assert \"hello admin\" in response.text.lower()\n\n\ndef test_public_home_answers(client):\n    response = client.get(\"/\")\n    assert response.status_code == 200\n\n\ndef test_publish_writes_a_site(tmp_path):\n    from app.publish import render_site\n\n    out = render_site(tmp_path / \"site\")\n    assert (out / \"index.html\").exists() and (out / \"style.css\").exists()\n\n\ndef test_published_html_uses_relative_paths(tmp_path):\n    \"\"\"Root-absolute paths break on GitHub Pages project URLs (rubric F2).\"\"\"\n    from app.publish import render_site\n\n    html = (render_site(tmp_path / \"site\") / \"index.html\").read_text()\n    assert 'href=\"/' not in html and 'src=\"/' not in html\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Create demo data so a grader (and you) can use the CMS immediately.\n\n    uv run python scripts/seed_demo.py\n\nT00 has nothing to seed. As you build content types, extend this so it creates:\n  - one admin and one editor (passwords read from .env, never hard-coded)\n  - a few posts and pages, at least one draft and one published\n\nThe rubric expects this to run clean on a fresh clone with .env.example values\n(item E4), because the database itself is never committed.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport sys\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    admin_pw = os.environ.get(\"CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD\")\n    editor_pw = os.environ.get(\"CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD\")\n    if not admin_pw or not editor_pw:\n        print(\"Set CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD and CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD in .env \"\n              \"(copy .env.example).\")\n        return 1\n\n    # TODO (your tickets): create the users, then the demo content.\n    print(\"Nothing to seed yet: no content types exist. \"\n          \"Extend scripts/seed_demo.py as you build T01+.\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n[project]\nname = \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]\naccessibility\tBarrier affecting people with disabilities\t#f143ab\nbug\tSomething isn't working\t#d73a4a\ndocumentation\tImprovements or additions to documentation\t#0075ca\nduplicate\tThis issue or pull request already exists\t#cfd3d7\nenhancement\tNew feature or request\t#a2eeef\ngood first issue\tGood for newcomers\t#7057ff\nhelp wanted\tExtra attention is needed\t#008672\ninvalid\tThis doesn't seem right\t#e4e669\nquestion\tFurther information is requested\t#d876e3\nwontfix\tThis will not be worked on\t#ffffff\nneeds-triage\t\t#0052CC\nneeds-info\t\t#5319E7\nready-for-agent\t\t#E99695\nready-for-human\t\t#B60205\nspec\t\t#FEF2C0\nticket\t\t#0E8A16","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"0840d2d2-a889-4f38-b41c-f45ba90a6794","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:46:16.429Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"# ADR-001: The admin console runs locally; the public site is a static export\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nA CMS needs a server, a database, and login. GitHub Pages serves static files\nonly: it cannot run Python and has no database. Students have two weeks and no\nhosting budget, and a first web application with real authentication is an\nattractive target the moment it is public.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe admin console (FastAPI + SQLite) runs on the student's own machine. A\n`cms publish` step renders the published content to static HTML in `site/`, which\nis deployed to GitHub Pages on the `gh-pages` branch.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Login, roles, and the database are never exposed to the internet.\n- The public site cannot have server-side features (search, comments) without a\n  different host; a stretch goal may add a live admin deploy behind a security gate.\n- Publishing is explicit: content changes are not live until `cms publish` runs,\n  which is a behaviour worth documenting for your client.\n- Relative paths become mandatory, because Pages serves the site from a subfolder.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (it shapes every route, template, and deploy step) and it\nis surprising without context (most CMSes are servers). That is the bar; most\ndecisions do not meet it and belong in the spec or the glossary instead.\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. Options\nconsidered: Django (batteries included, but a large amount of generated code to\nread), Flask (similar to FastAPI here), a JavaScript stack (a second language and\na build step), and FastAPI with server-rendered templates.\n\n## Decision\n\nFastAPI with Jinja2 templates, SQLite for storage, argon2 for password hashing,\nand an HTML sanitizer for user Markdown. No frontend framework, no build step.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Every page is server-rendered, so \"view source\" shows what the code produced,\n  which makes debugging visible.\n- SQLite is a single file, so a grader can seed and inspect it easily. It is\n  gitignored; `scripts/seed_demo.py` rebuilds it.\n- No build step means the file you preview is the file that ships.\n- Interactive flourishes (live preview without reload, drag-and-drop) cost more\n  here than in a JavaScript stack. That is an accepted trade.\n# ADR-003: Editors write at the Sunday meetings; only Admins Publish\n\n**Status:** accepted\n**Date:** 2026-10-24\n\n## Context\n\nThe assignment requires two roles, Admin and Editor, with different\npermissions. ADR-001 keeps the admin console on one local machine, the\nPresident's laptop, which never goes on the internet. So an Editor account is\nonly useful if members can get to that laptop. The officers also want to\nreview and edit every Recap before readers see it.\n\n## Decision\n\n- **Editors exist.** Members the President and VP choose get an Editor\n  account, created by an Admin. An Editor writes and edits their own Recaps\n  as Drafts and adds Images with alt text. They cannot Publish or Unpublish,\n  create Trips, manage accounts, Deploy, make Backups, or edit another\n  person's Recap.\n- **Publish is Admin-only.** Admins (President and VP) can do everything an\n  Editor can, plus all of the above, and can edit any Recap.\n- **Editors write at the Sunday club meetings.** The President brings the\n  Console laptop, so members don't have to track an officer down across\n  campus. A member who misses a meeting can email their writing to an Admin,\n  who enters it for them as a Contributor.\n\n## Why Publish stays Admin-only\n\nNothing is public until an Admin Deploys, so letting Editors Publish would\nnot make anything go live sooner. It would only blur which Recaps an Admin has\nactually checked. With Publish kept for Admins, Published means exactly one\nthing: an Admin approved this Recap.\n\n## Considered and rejected\n\n- **No Editor accounts, with Admins entering everything.** Simpler, but it\n  fails the assignment's two-role requirement.\n- **Remote editing, so Editors log in from their own laptops.** This needs the\n  console hosted on the internet, with the security work ADR-001 avoids. Out\n  of scope.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- This departs from the brief's \"editors can publish their own posts\". The\n  Editor role here matches WordPress's Contributor role more than its Editor\n  role.\n- Writing happens on a weekly rhythm. A Recap written between meetings waits\n  for Sunday, or goes through an Admin.\n\"\"\"The FastAPI application.\n\nT00 (already done): the admin console answers at /admin and the public site\nanswers at /. That is the whole skeleton — it exists so you can prove the stack\nruns before you build anything on it.\n\nAdd your routes in their own modules (app/routes/posts.py and so on) and include\nthem here. Keep this file small.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, Request\nfrom fastapi.templating import Jinja2Templates\n\nfrom app import settings\n\ntemplates = Jinja2Templates(directory=str(settings.TEMPLATES))\n\n\ndef create_app() -> FastAPI:\n    app = FastAPI(title=\"IPHS 400 MP2 CMS\")\n\n    @app.get(\"/admin\")\n    def admin_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"admin/hello.html\", {\"title\": \"Admin\"}\n        )\n\n    @app.get(\"/\")\n    def public_home(request: Request):\n        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n            request, \"public/home.html\",\n            {\"title\": settings.SITE_TITLE, \"items\": []},\n        )\n\n    # Your ticket work plugs in here, e.g.\n    #   from app.routes import posts\n    #   app.include_router(posts.router)\n    return app\n\n\napp = create_app()\n\"\"\"The `cms` command: serve, publish, deploy.\n\n    uv run cms serve      # admin console + public preview at http://localhost:8000\n    uv run cms publish    # render site/ from published content\n    uv run cms deploy     # push site/ to the gh-pages branch (Pages serves it)\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfrom app import settings\nfrom app.publish import render_site\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"cms\")\n    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"command\", required=True)\n    serve = sub.add_parser(\"serve\", help=\"run the admin console locally\")\n    serve.add_argument(\"--port\", type=int, default=8000)\n    serve.add_argument(\"--reload\", action=\"store_true\", default=True)\n    sub.add_parser(\"publish\", help=\"render site/ from published content\")\n    deploy = sub.add_parser(\"deploy\", help=\"push site/ to gh-pages\")\n    deploy.add_argument(\"--message\", default=\"Publish site\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    if args.command == \"serve\":\n        import uvicorn\n\n        uvicorn.run(\"app.main:app\", port=args.port, reload=args.reload)\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"publish\":\n        out = render_site()\n        print(f\"Wrote {out}. Preview it with:  python3 -m http.server -d {out} 8001\")\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"deploy\":\n        if not settings.SITE.exists():\n            print(\"site/ does not exist yet — run `uv run cms publish` first.\")\n            return 1\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [sys.executable, \"-m\", \"ghp_import\", \"-n\", \"-p\", \"-m\", args.message,\n             str(settings.SITE)],\n        )\n        if result.returncode == 0:\n            print(\"Pushed to gh-pages. Settings -> Pages -> Deploy from a branch -> \"\n                  \"gh-pages / root, then wait up to 10 minutes.\")\n        return result.returncode\n    return 1\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n\nT00 publishes a placeholder home page. A draft that reaches site/ is a bug.\n  2. Passwords come from the environment there; in\n# tests they are fixed and meaningless.\nDEMO_USERS = {\n    \"admin\": {\"email\": \"admin@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-admin-pw\"},\n    \"editor\": {\"email\": \"editor@example.test\", \"password\": \"test-editor-pw\"},\n}\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client() -> TestClient:\n    return TestClient(create_app())\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client_as():\n    \"\"\"Return a factory: client_as(\"editor\") -> a logged-in TestClient.\"\"\"\n\n    def _login(role: str) -> TestClient:\n        user = DEMO_USERS[role]\n        c = TestClient(create_app())\n        response = c.post(\"/login\", data={\"email\": user[\"email\"],\n                                          \"password\": user[\"password\"]},\n                          follow_redirects=False)\n        if response.status_code == 404:\n            pytest.skip(\"No /login route yet — build the login ticket first.\")\n        assert response.status_code in (200, 302, 303), (\n            f\"Login as {role} failed with {response.status_code}\")\n        return c\n\n    return _login\n\"\"\"T00: the walking skeleton. These pass in the fresh template.\n\nDo not delete them. {"parentUuid":"fe2fb1de-28f2-4c58-a78a-ecdbac775830","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"5be03593-c62d-4407-9739-50e194039a0b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TXcBXkUjLwajC45Zsudvz8","type":"tool_result","content":"Vocabulary follows `CONTEXT.md`. Decisions behind it: ADR-001 (local admin console, static Public site), ADR-002 (stack), ADR-003 (Editors write at the Sunday meetings; only Admins Publish).\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe Kenyon Model UN club has no website. Trip recaps go on Instagram, where they sink in the feed, so nobody in the Kenyon community can look back at a past Trip. The President and Vice President want a place where members' Recaps of each Trip (where the team went, how the competition went, what they explored and ate) are published with photos and stay findable. Members need to be able to write their own Recaps. The officers must stay in control of what reaches readers, and no member can ever change another member's work or touch accounts.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS with two halves:\n\n- An **admin console** that runs only on the **Console laptop**. Admins and Editors log in to it. Editors write their own Recaps at the Sunday club meetings and Submit them. Admins review, edit, Send back, or Publish them. Admins also manage Trips, Pages, and Accounts, and enter Recaps for Contributors who email their writing in.\n- A static **Public site** that an Admin Deploys from the terminal. It lists Trips newest first. Each Trip page shows its Summary and then its Published Recaps. Admin-written Pages, such as \"About the club\", form the Navigation bar. A footer link sends readers to Instagram for discussion. Only Published content reaches the Public site, and all of it is sanitized.\n\nAn Admin can also take a dated Backup of the database and Images with `cms backup`, so losing the laptop doesn't lose the club's writing.\n\n## User Stories\n\n**Login and Accounts**\n\n1. As an Admin, I want to log in with my email and password, so that only I can use Admin powers on the Console laptop.\n2. As an Editor, I want to log in with my own Account at the Sunday meeting, so that my Recaps are mine and nobody else's.\n3. As any logged-in person, I want to sign out, so that the next member at the laptop can't act as me.\n4. As an anonymous visitor to the console, I want to be redirected to the login page from every console page, so that nothing in the console is reachable without an Account.\n5. As an Admin, I want passwords stored only as argon2 hashes, so that a copied database doesn't reveal anyone's password.\n6. As a person who typed a wrong password, I want a generic \"email or password is incorrect\" message, so that the login page doesn't reveal which emails have Accounts.\n7. As an Admin, I want a Deactivated Account to be unable to log in, and any session it already has to stop working, so that a member who leaves the club loses access immediately.\n\n**Roles and permissions**\n\n8. As an Admin, I want Editors to get a 403 or a redirect on every Admin-only page and action, so that the Editor role is genuinely limited.\n9. As an Editor, I want to create Recaps, edit and delete my own Drafts, add Images to them, and Submit them, so that I can contribute without waiting on an officer.\n10. As an Admin, I want an Editor to be unable to Publish or Unpublish anything, so that Published always means an Admin approved it.\n11. As an Editor, I want to be refused when I try to view, edit, Submit, or delete another person's Recap, so that nobody can tamper with my writing.\n12. As an Editor, I want my Recap locked against my own edits once it is Submitted or Published, so that what an Admin reviewed doesn't change underneath them.\n13. As an Admin, I want to do everything an Editor can, and also edit or delete any Recap, so that I can fix typos and take down anything inappropriate.\n\n**Trips**\n\n14. As an Admin, I want to create a Trip with a conference name, host city, start date, end date, and Summary, so that Recaps have something to belong to.\n15. As an Admin, I want the Trip's Slug suggested from its year and city (such as `2026-chicago`) and editable, so that addresses are readable.\n16. As an Admin, I want to edit any Trip, so that I can correct its Summary or dates later.\n17. As an Admin, I want deleting a Trip refused while it still has Recaps, so that Recaps are never lost along with their Trip.\n18. As an Editor, I want to pick the Trip my Recap is about from a list, so that I can't misspell or invent a Trip.\n\n**Recaps**\n\n19. As an Editor, I want to write a Recap with a title, a Slug, and a Markdown Body, so that I can tell my story with basic formatting.\n20. As an Editor, I want the Slug suggested from the title, so that I don't have to think about URLs.\n21. As an Admin, I want a Recap's Slug to be unique within its Trip, with a clear error on a clash, so that two Recaps never share an address.\n22. As an Editor, I want my Byline (name and class year) filled in from my Account, so that I'm credited correctly without typing it.\n23. As an Admin entering a Contributor's emailed Recap, I want to type their name and class year as the Byline, so that the Contributor is credited and not me.\n24. As an Admin, I want every Recap to record its Owner and when it was created, last edited, and Published, so that I know who is responsible for it and how fresh it is.\n25. As an Admin, I want saving or Submitting a Recap with no Body text to be refused, so that no Recap is just photos.\n26. As an Editor, I want to Submit my finished Draft, so that the Admins know it's ready to review.\n27. As an Admin, I want to Send back a Submitted Recap to Draft, so that its Editor can revise it.\n28. As an Admin, I want to Publish a Draft or Submitted Recap, so that it goes out with the next Deploy.\n29. As an Admin, I want to Unpublish a Published Recap back to Draft, so that I can take it down at the next Deploy.\n30. As an Admin editing or Unpublishing a Published Recap or Page, I want a notice that the change is \"still live until you Deploy\", so that I don't assume readers already see the change.\n31. As an Editor, I want to delete my own Recap while it is a Draft, so that I can abandon a false start.\n\n**Images**\n\n32. As an Editor, I want to upload a JPEG, PNG, or WebP photo of up to about 5 MB to my Recap, with required alt text, so that my Recap has pictures that screen readers can describe.\n33. As an Editor, I want the console to give me a ready-to-paste Markdown snippet with my alt text filled in after an upload, so that I can place the photo where I want it in my Body.\n34. As an Admin, I want uploads of any other file type, or larger files, refused with a clear message, so that the Public site doesn't fill up with huge or unsafe files.\n35. As an Admin, I want Publish refused while the Body has an image without alt text, so that the Public site stays accessible.\n36. As an Admin, I want a Recap's Images deleted along with the Recap, so that orphaned photos don't pile up.\n\n**Pages and Navigation bar**\n\n37. As an Admin, I want to create, edit, and delete Pages (title, Slug, text-only Markdown Body, Draft or Published), so that the club has an \"About\" page and an \"Officers and contact\" page.\n38. As an Admin, I want each Page to record its Owner and when it was created and last edited, so that Pages are accountable like Recaps.\n39. As an Admin, I want to set a number for each Page's position in the Navigation bar, so that I control the order of the links.\n40. As an Admin, I want a Page Slug refused if it is reserved (`trips`, `images`, `admin`, `login`) or already used by another Page, so that addresses never collide.\n41. As an Editor, I want the Pages area to be closed to me, so that the role boundary is obvious.\n\n**Admin console**\n\n42. As an Admin, I want a dashboard showing counts of Recaps by status (Draft, Submitted, Published), Trips, Pages by status, and active Accounts, so that I can see at a glance what needs attention.\n43. As an Editor, I want my dashboard to show counts of my own Recaps by status, so that I know what I still owe.\n44. As an Admin, I want a \"changes not yet live\" notice on the dashboard whenever content has changed since the last `cms publish`, so that I remember to Deploy.\n45. As an Admin, I want a content list of Recaps that I can filter by status, by Trip, and by Owner, with Submitted Recaps shown first, so that I can find what to review quickly.\n46. As an Editor, I want the content list to show only my own Recaps, filterable by status and Trip, so that I see my own work and nobody else's.\n47. As an Admin, I want a Pages list I can filter by status, so that I can find Drafts.\n48. As an Editor or Admin, I want a Preview button on the Recap and Page editor that reloads the page showing the sanitized, rendered Body exactly as readers will see it, so that I can check formatting and Image placement before saving or Submitting.\n49. As any console user, I want every form to carry a CSRF token, and any submission without a valid one refused, so that another website can't make my browser act for me.\n\n**Account management (Admin only)**\n\n50. As an Admin, I want to create an Account with an email, name, class year, role, and initial password, so that the members the President and VP chose can log in.\n51. As an Admin, I want to change an Account's role between Editor and Admin, so that officer changes can be handled.\n52. As an Admin, I want to Deactivate an Account while its Recaps, Pages, and Bylines stay, so that departing members lose access without their writing disappearing.\n53. As an Admin, I want Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin refused, so that the club can never lock itself out.\n54. As outgoing officers at Handover, we want to create the incoming Admins' Accounts and have them Deactivate ours, so that control transfers cleanly each year.\n\n**Public site and Deploy**\n\n55. As a Kenyon reader, I want the home page to list Trips newest first, so that I see the latest Trip immediately and can scroll back to older ones.\n56. As a reader, I want each Trip's page to show its Summary and then its Published Recaps in the order they were Published, with their Bylines, so that I get the overview and then each member's story.\n57. As a reader, I want a Trip to appear only once it has at least one Published Recap, so that I never land on an empty Trip.\n58. As a reader, I want a Navigation bar with Home first, then the Published Pages in the order the Admins set, so that \"About the club\" is always one click away.\n59. As a reader, I want a footer link to the club's Instagram for discussion, so that I can react to a Recap even though the site has no comments.\n60. As an Admin, I want `cms publish` to write only Published Recaps and Pages, and only Trips that have a Published Recap, into `site/`, so that Drafts and Submitted Recaps never leak.\n61. As an Admin, I want every Body sanitized when it is rendered, with scripts and raw HTML stripped and external links and external images removed, so that nothing harmful or off-site reaches readers.\n62. As an Admin, I want every link and image in `site/` to be a relative path, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages subfolder.\n63. As an Admin, I want only the Images of Published Recaps copied into `site/`, so that photos from Drafts never become public.\n64. As an Admin, I want `cms deploy` to push `site/` live, so that a Deploy is `cms publish` followed by `cms deploy`.\n\n**Backup and seed data**\n\n65. As an Admin, I want `cms backup` to write one dated archive containing the database and all uploaded Images to a folder I choose outside the repository, so that I can keep a private copy on the club's Drive.\n66. As an Admin, I want `cms backup` to refuse to write inside the repository, so that a Backup can never be committed by accident.\n67. As a grader, I want the seed script to create one Admin and one Editor (passwords from the environment), a Trip, Recaps in each status, and a Draft and a Published Page, so that I can try every capability on a fresh clone.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Storage.** SQLite through the standard library, at the existing database path setting. The schema:\n\n- **Account**: email (unique, used to log in), name, class year, role (`admin` or `editor`), argon2 password hash, active flag, created timestamp.\n- **Trip**: conference name, host city, start date, end date, Summary, Slug (unique), created and edited timestamps.\n- **Recap**: Trip, Owner (an Account), title, Slug (unique per Trip), Body (Markdown), Byline name, Byline class year, status (`draft`, `submitted`, `published`), and created, edited, and Published timestamps.\n- **Page**: Owner, title, Slug (unique), Body (Markdown), status (`draft`, `published`), nav position, and created and edited timestamps.\n- **Image**: Recap, stored file name, original type, size, alt text.\n- **Site state**: a single record holding when `cms publish` last ran, which the \"changes not yet live\" notice uses.\n\n**Byline.** The Byline is copied onto the Recap when it is created: from the Owner's Account, or typed by an Admin for a Contributor. Admins can edit it. Renaming or Deactivating an Account later doesn't rewrite existing Bylines.\n\n**Recap status machine.** Every Recap starts as a Draft.\n\n| From | To | Who |\n|---|---|---|\n| Draft | Submitted | the Editor who Owns it |\n| Submitted | Draft (Send back) | Admin |\n| Draft or Submitted | Published | Admin |\n| Published | Draft (Unpublish) | Admin |\n\nEditors may edit or delete only their own Drafts. Admins may edit or delete any Recap. Pages go between Draft and Published, and only Admins move them.\n\n**Permission policy.** A single module answers \"can this Account do this action to this object\". Every route asks it, so there are no ad-hoc role checks scattered across routes. Being logged out redirects to login. Being logged in but not allowed returns 403. The policy rereads the Account on every request, so a Deactivation or role change takes effect at once.\n\n**Login and sessions.** Login takes an email and password at `/login`, matching the existing `client_as` test fixture. Sessions use a signed cookie through the existing `itsdangerous` dependency. Sign out clears the session. Login failures show one generic message.\n\n**CSRF.** Each session has a token, rendered as a hidden field in every form that changes state. Any state-changing request without a matching token gets a 403. No new dependency.\n\n**Markdown rendering.** One function turns a Body into HTML: `markdown-it-py`, then `nh3` with an allowlist of formatting tags. It removes:\n- any link whose target is not relative,\n- any image whose source is not one of the Recap's own uploaded Images,\n- all raw HTML.\n\nPreview and `cms publish` both use this same function, so what Preview shows is what readers get.\n\n**Images.** Accepted types are JPEG, PNG, and WebP, checked by file content and not by extension, up to about 5 MB. Images are stored in an uploads folder that is gitignored and outside `site/`. The snippet the console offers is a relative `images/<file>` reference. `cms publish` copies a Published Recap's Images next to its page, so that relative reference resolves.\n\n**Admin console screens.**\n- Login.\n- Dashboard, with counts and the \"changes not yet live\" notice.\n- Recaps list with filters for status, Trip, and Owner (Owner for Admins only), Submitted first for Admins.\n- Recap editor with Preview, Image upload, and action buttons for the transitions above.\n- Trips list and editor.\n- Pages list (filter by status) and editor with Preview and nav position.\n- Accounts list and editor.\n\nAll console routes sit under `/admin`, except `/login` and `/logout`. Screens are server-rendered with Jinja. There is no JavaScript framework and no live preview (ADR-002).\n\n**Admin-only routes.** These must refuse Editors:\n- Trip create, edit, and delete.\n- All Page routes.\n- All Account routes.\n- Publish, Unpublish, and Send back.\n- Editing, Submitting, or deleting a Recap the Editor doesn't Own.\n\n**Public site rendering.** `render_site` writes:\n- the home page (Trips with a Published Recap, newest start date first),\n- one page per such Trip,\n- one page per Published Recap,\n- one page per Published Page,\n- the stylesheet,\n- copied Images.\n\nAddresses take the forms `trips/<trip>/`, `trips/<trip>/<recap>/` and `<page>/`, each as an index file. Every link is computed relative to the page's depth. The Navigation bar is Home followed by the Published Pages by nav position. The footer holds the Instagram link, taken from a constant committed in the repository's settings (not an environment variable), so `cms publish` produces the same footer on any machine with no setup. Its value is the placeholder `https://www.instagram.com/REPLACE-ME/` for now. Nothing that isn't Published is ever read by the renderer.\n\n**Deploy and Backup run from the terminal only.** Deploy is `cms publish` followed by `cms deploy`, both of which already exist. The console has no Deploy or Backup button. \"Only Admins Deploy and Backup\" holds because the terminal belongs to the President.\n\n**`cms backup`.** A new subcommand on the existing CLI. It takes the SQLite online-backup copy of the database and the uploads folder, and zips them with the standard library into `cms-backup-<YYYYMMDD-HHMM>.zip` in an output folder given as an argument. It refuses any output folder inside the repository. Backup archives are also gitignored as a second safeguard.\n\n**Seed data.** Extend the existing seed script. Its Admin and Editor emails match the test fixture, and passwords come from `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`. It creates demo content in every status.\n\n**`CONTEXT.md`.** The Deploy entry now says that a Deploy is `cms publish` (render) plus `cms deploy` (push). It is corrected in the same commit that references this spec.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\nA good test drives the system from outside and checks what a user or reader would see: HTTP status codes, redirects, page text, and files written to `site/`. Tests don't reach into tables or private functions. They use only the seams that already exist, and no new seams are added.\n\n**Seam 1: the HTTP app.** Tests use `TestClient` with the `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures. The fixtures may be extended so each test gets a temporary database with the demo Accounts seeded. That changes how the fixtures are set up, not which seam the tests use. The required tests are:\n\n- **Anonymous visitors**: requesting every console route redirects to login.\n- **Editors on Admin-only routes**: a table-driven test hits every Admin-only route and action listed above as an Editor and expects a 403 or a redirect for each one.\n- **Editors cannot Publish**: an Editor's Publish and Unpublish requests are refused, and the Recap's status stays the same.\n- **Editors cannot touch another person's Recap**: edit, Submit, and delete requests on someone else's Recap are refused, and the Recap is unchanged.\n- **Editors are locked out after Submit**: an Editor can't edit their own Recap once it is Submitted or Published.\n- **Passwords**: the stored password is an argon2 hash and never the plain text. Logging in with the right password works, and with a wrong one fails with the generic message. Signing out ends the session.\n- **Deactivation**: a Deactivated Account can't log in, and its existing session stops working. Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused.\n- **Role changes**: after an Admin changes an Account's role, its permissions follow the new role.\n- **CSRF**: every state-changing form contains a token, and a POST without a valid token gets a 403.\n- **Recap rules**: Submit, Send back, Publish, and Unpublish each move the status as specified. An empty Body is refused. A Slug that clashes within its Trip is refused. Deleting a Trip that has Recaps is refused.\n- **Image rules**: wrong file types, oversized files, and missing alt text are refused. Publish is refused while the Body has an image without alt text.\n- **Dashboard**: the counts match the seeded data, for both an Admin and an Editor.\n- **Content list**: the status, Trip, and Owner filters return the right Recaps. Editors see only their own Recaps.\n- **Preview**: given a Body containing `<script>`, an external link, and an external image, Preview shows sanitized HTML with all three removed.\n\n**Seam 2: `render_site(out)` into `tmp_path`.** These tests follow the prior art in `tests/test_t00.py`. Using a seeded database, they check:\n\n- Draft and Submitted Recaps, Draft Pages, and Trips with no Published Recap are absent from the output.\n- Published content is present.\n- Body sanitization holds in the output.\n- The Navigation bar lists Home, then the Published Pages in nav order.\n- No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears anywhere.\n- Only the Images of Published Recaps are copied.\n\n**Existing CLI entry point: `main(argv)`.** `cms backup` writes a dated archive containing the database and the uploads, and refuses an output folder inside the repository.\n\nThe tests in `tests/test_t00.py` must keep passing.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Comments on the Public site. This is a known gap, and the Instagram footer link stands in for it.\n- Images on Pages, as a possible later addition. Pages are text-only.\n- A hosted or remote admin console. Editors use the Console laptop (ADR-001, ADR-003).\n- Deploy and Backup buttons in the console.\n- Reactivating a Deactivated Account. Admins create a new Account instead (see Further Notes).\n- A live preview, a WYSIWYG editor, drag-and-drop, multi-site, tags and categories, and search.\n- Password reset by email. An Admin sets a new password instead.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n**Coverage of the seven required capabilities**\n\n| # | Capability | Where this spec covers it |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Login: sign in and out, hashed passwords | Stories 1–7. Login, sessions and argon2 decisions. Password and Deactivation tests. |\n| 2 | Two roles with genuinely different permissions | Stories 8–13 and 41. Permission policy. Admin-only route list. Required 403-or-redirect and Recap-ownership tests. |\n| 3 | Posts (here, Recaps and Trips): CRUD, title, Slug, Markdown Body, draft and published, author, timestamps | Stories 14–31. Schema (Owner as author, Byline as the displayed author). Status machine. |\n| 4 | Pages, with published Pages in the nav | Stories 37–41 and 58. Page schema. Navigation bar rendering. |\n| 5 | Admin console: dashboard counts, filterable content list, editor with Preview | Stories 42–49. Console screens. Dashboard, content list and Preview tests. |\n| 6 | User management: Admin-only, create, change role, Deactivate, Deactivated users can't log in | Stories 50–54. Account schema and policy. Deactivation and role tests. |\n| 7 | Public site and publish: only published content, sanitized Markdown, nav from Pages, `cms publish` writes `site/` with relative paths | Stories 55–64. Rendering and sanitizer decisions. Seam 2 tests. |\n\n**Reactivation.** Reactivating a Deactivated Account never came up while the design was settled. This spec leaves it out to keep the Account rules small. Raise it before ticketing if the officers want it.\n\n**Instagram handle.** The footer link is a placeholder, `https://www.instagram.com/REPLACE-ME/`. The owner will replace it with the club's real Instagram handle before the final publish.\n\n**Who can Deploy.** \"Only Admins Deploy and Backup\" is enforced by the Console laptop's terminal being the President's, not by code. This is a deliberate trade-off, made in the spec session.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"b75189b2-d42f-4663-948b-44456c813a13","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:46:29.042Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"Vocabulary follows `CONTEXT.md`. Decisions behind it: ADR-001 (local admin console, static Public site), ADR-002 (stack), ADR-003 (Editors write at the Sunday meetings; only Admins Publish).\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe Kenyon Model UN club has no website. Trip recaps go on Instagram, where they sink in the feed, so nobody in the Kenyon community can look back at a past Trip. The President and Vice President want a place where members' Recaps of each Trip (where the team went, how the competition went, what they explored and ate) are published with photos and stay findable. Members need to be able to write their own Recaps. The officers must stay in control of what reaches readers, and no member can ever change another member's work or touch accounts.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS with two halves:\n\n- An **admin console** that runs only on the **Console laptop**. Admins and Editors log in to it. Editors write their own Recaps at the Sunday club meetings and Submit them. Admins review, edit, Send back, or Publish them. Admins also manage Trips, Pages, and Accounts, and enter Recaps for Contributors who email their writing in.\n- A static **Public site** that an Admin Deploys from the terminal. A footer link sends readers to Instagram for discussion. As an Admin, I want a dashboard showing counts of Recaps by status (Draft, Submitted, Published), Trips, Pages by status, and active Accounts, so that I can see at a glance what needs attention.\n43. As an Editor, I want my dashboard to show counts of my own Recaps by status, so that I know what I still owe.\n44. As an Admin, I want a \"changes not yet live\" notice on the dashboard whenever content has changed since the last `cms publish`, so that I remember to Deploy.\n45. As an Admin, I want a content list of Recaps that I can filter by status, by Trip, and by Owner, with Submitted Recaps shown first, so that I can find what to review quickly.\n46. As an Editor, I want the content list to show only my own Recaps, filterable by status and Trip, so that I see my own work and nobody else's.\n47. As an Admin, I want a Pages list I can filter by status, so that I can find Drafts.\n48. As an Editor or Admin, I want a Preview button on the Recap and Page editor that reloads the page showing the sanitized, rendered Body exactly as readers will see it, so that I can check formatting and Image placement before saving or Submitting.\n49. As any console user, I want every form to carry a CSRF token, and any submission without a valid one refused, so that another website can't make my browser act for me.\n\n**Account management (Admin only)**\n\n50. As an Admin, I want to create an Account with an email, name, class year, role, and initial password, so that the members the President and VP chose can log in.\n51. As an Admin, I want to change an Account's role between Editor and Admin, so that officer changes can be handled.\n52. As an Admin, I want to Deactivate an Account while its Recaps, Pages, and Bylines stay, so that departing members lose access without their writing disappearing.\n53. As an Admin, I want Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin refused, so that the club can never lock itself out.\n54. As outgoing officers at Handover, we want to create the incoming Admins' Accounts and have them Deactivate ours, so that control transfers cleanly each year.\n\n**Public site and Deploy**\n\n55. As a Kenyon reader, I want the home page to list Trips newest first, so that I see the latest Trip immediately and can scroll back to older ones.\n56. As a reader, I want each Trip's page to show its Summary and then its Published Recaps in the order they were Published, with their Bylines, so that I get the overview and then each member's story.\n57. As a reader, I want a Trip to appear only once it has at least one Published Recap, so that I never land on an empty Trip.\n58. As a reader, I want a Navigation bar with Home first, then the Published Pages in the order the Admins set, so that \"About the club\" is always one click away.\n59. As a reader, I want a footer link to the club's Instagram for discussion, so that I can react to a Recap even though the site has no comments.\n60. As an Admin, I want `cms publish` to write only Published Recaps and Pages, and only Trips that have a Published Recap, into `site/`, so that Drafts and Submitted Recaps never leak.\n61. As an Admin, I want every Body sanitized when it is rendered, with scripts and raw HTML stripped and external links and external images removed, so that nothing harmful or off-site reaches readers.\n62. As an Admin, I want every link and image in `site/` to be a relative path, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages subfolder.\n63. As an Admin, I want only the Images of Published Recaps copied into `site/`, so that photos from Drafts never become public.\n64. As an Admin, I want `cms deploy` to push `site/` live, so that a Deploy is `cms publish` followed by `cms deploy`.\n\n**Backup and seed data**\n\n65. As an Admin, I want `cms backup` to write one dated archive containing the database and all uploaded Images to a folder I choose outside the repository, so that I can keep a private copy on the club's Drive.\n66. As an Admin, I want `cms backup` to refuse to write inside the repository, so that a Backup can never be committed by accident.\n67. As a grader, I want the seed script to create one Admin and one Editor (passwords from the environment), a Trip, Recaps in each status, and a Draft and a Published Page, so that I can try every capability on a fresh clone.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Storage.** SQLite through the standard library, at the existing database path setting. The schema:\n\n- **Account**: email (unique, used to log in), name, class year, role (`admin` or `editor`), argon2 password hash, active flag, created timestamp.\n- **Trip**: conference name, host city, start date, end date, Summary, Slug (unique), created and edited timestamps.\n- **Recap**: Trip, Owner (an Account), title, Slug (unique per Trip), Body (Markdown), Byline name, Byline class year, status (`draft`, `submitted`, `published`), and created, edited, and Published timestamps.\n- **Page**: Owner, title, Slug (unique), Body (Markdown), status (`draft`, `published`), nav position, and created and edited timestamps.\n- **Image**: Recap, stored file name, original type, size, alt text.\n- **Site state**: a single record holding when `cms publish` last ran, which the \"changes not yet live\" notice uses.\n\n**Byline.** The Byline is copied onto the Recap when it is created: from the Owner's Account, or typed by an Admin for a Contributor. Admins can edit it. Renaming or Deactivating an Account later doesn't rewrite existing Bylines.\n\n**Recap status machine.** Every Recap starts as a Draft.\n\n| From | To | Who |\n|---|---|---|\n| Draft | Submitted | the Editor who Owns it |\n| Submitted | Draft (Send back) | Admin |\n| Draft or Submitted | Published | Admin |\n| Published | Draft (Unpublish) | Admin |\n\nEditors may edit or delete only their own Drafts. Admins may edit or delete any Recap. Pages go between Draft and Published, and only Admins move them.\n\n**Permission policy.** A single module answers \"can this Account do this action to this object\". Every route asks it, so there are no ad-hoc role checks scattered across routes. Being logged out redirects to login. Being logged in but not allowed returns 403. The policy rereads the Account on every request, so a Deactivation or role change takes effect at once.\n\n**Login and sessions.** Login takes an email and password at `/login`, matching the existing `client_as` test fixture. Sessions use a signed cookie through the existing `itsdangerous` dependency. Sign out clears the session. Login failures show one generic message.\n\n**CSRF.** Each session has a token, rendered as a hidden field in every form that changes state. Any state-changing request without a matching token gets a 403. No new dependency.\n\n**Markdown rendering.** One function turns a Body into HTML: `markdown-it-py`, then `nh3` with an allowlist of formatting tags. It removes:\n- any link whose target is not relative,\n- any image whose source is not one of the Recap's own uploaded Images,\n- all raw HTML.\n\nPreview and `cms publish` both use this same function, so what Preview shows is what readers get.\n\n**Images.** Accepted types are JPEG, PNG, and WebP, checked by file content and not by extension, up to about 5 MB. Images are stored in an uploads folder that is gitignored and outside `site/`. The snippet the console offers is a relative `images/<file>` reference. `cms publish` copies a Published Recap's Images next to its page, so that relative reference resolves.\n\n**Admin console screens.**\n- Login.\n- Dashboard, with counts and the \"changes not yet live\" notice.\n- Recaps list with filters for status, Trip, and Owner (Owner for Admins only), Submitted first for Admins.\n- Recap editor with Preview, Image upload, and action buttons for the transitions above.\n- Trips list and editor.\n- Pages list (filter by status) and editor with Preview and nav position.\n- Accounts list and editor.\n\nAll console routes sit under `/admin`, except `/login` and `/logout`. Screens are server-rendered with Jinja. There is no JavaScript framework and no live preview (ADR-002).\n\n**Admin-only routes.** These must refuse Editors:\n- Trip create, edit, and delete.\n- All Page routes.\n- All Account routes.\n- Publish, Unpublish, and Send back.\n- Editing, Submitting, or deleting a Recap the Editor doesn't Own.\n\n**Public site rendering.** `render_site` writes:\n- the home page (Trips with a Published Recap, newest start date first),\n- one page per such Trip,\n- one page per Published Recap,\n- one page per Published Page,\n- the stylesheet,\n- copied Images.\n\nAddresses take the forms `trips/<trip>/`, `trips/<trip>/<recap>/` and `<page>/`, each as an index file. Every link is computed relative to the page's depth. The Navigation bar is Home followed by the Published Pages by nav position. The footer holds the Instagram link, taken from a constant committed in the repository's settings (not an environment variable), so `cms publish` produces the same footer on any machine with no setup. Its value is the placeholder `https://www.instagram.com/REPLACE-ME/` for now. Nothing that isn't Published is ever read by the renderer.\n\n**Deploy and Backup run from the terminal only.** Deploy is `cms publish` followed by `cms deploy`, both of which already exist. The console has no Deploy or Backup button. \"Only Admins Deploy and Backup\" holds because the terminal belongs to the President.\n\n**`cms backup`.** A new subcommand on the existing CLI. It takes the SQLite online-backup copy of the database and the uploads folder, and zips them with the standard library into `cms-backup-<YYYYMMDD-HHMM>.zip` in an output folder given as an argument. It refuses any output folder inside the repository. Backup archives are also gitignored as a second safeguard.\n\n**Seed data.** Extend the existing seed script. Its Admin and Editor emails match the test fixture, and passwords come from `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`. It creates demo content in every status.\n\n**`CONTEXT.md`.** The Deploy entry now says that a Deploy is `cms publish` (render) plus `cms deploy` (push). It is corrected in the same commit that references this spec.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\nA good test drives the system from outside and checks what a user or reader would see: HTTP status codes, redirects, page text, and files written to `site/`. Tests don't reach into tables or private functions. They use only the seams that already exist, and no new seams are added.\n\n**Seam 1: the HTTP app.** Tests use `TestClient` with the `client` and `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")` fixtures. The fixtures may be extended so each test gets a temporary database with the demo Accounts seeded. That changes how the fixtures are set up, not which seam the tests use. The required tests are:\n\n- **Anonymous visitors**: requesting every console route redirects to login.\n- **Editors on Admin-only routes**: a table-driven test hits every Admin-only route and action listed above as an Editor and expects a 403 or a redirect for each one.\n- **Editors cannot Publish**: an Editor's Publish and Unpublish requests are refused, and the Recap's status stays the same.\n- **Editors cannot touch another person's Recap**: edit, Submit, and delete requests on someone else's Recap are refused, and the Recap is unchanged.\n- **Editors are locked out after Submit**: an Editor can't edit their own Recap once it is Submitted or Published.\n- **Passwords**: the stored password is an argon2 hash and never the plain text. Logging in with the right password works, and with a wrong one fails with the generic message. Signing out ends the session.\n- **Deactivation**: a Deactivated Account can't log in, and its existing session stops working. Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused.\n- **Role changes**: after an Admin changes an Account's role, its permissions follow the new role.\n- **CSRF**: every state-changing form contains a token, and a POST without a valid token gets a 403.\n- **Recap rules**: Submit, Send back, Publish, and Unpublish each move the status as specified. An empty Body is refused. A Slug that clashes within its Trip is refused. Deleting a Trip that has Recaps is refused.\n- **Image rules**: wrong file types, oversized files, and missing alt text are refused. Publish is refused while the Body has an image without alt text.\n- **Dashboard**: the counts match the seeded data, for both an Admin and an Editor.\n- **Content list**: the status, Trip, and Owner filters return the right Recaps. Editors see only their own Recaps.\n- **Preview**: given a Body containing `<script>`, an external link, and an external image, Preview shows sanitized HTML with all three removed.\n\n**Seam 2: `render_site(out)` into `tmp_path`.** These tests follow the prior art in `tests/test_t00.py`. Using a seeded database, they check:\n\n- Draft and Submitted Recaps, Draft Pages, and Trips with no Published Recap are absent from the output.\n- Published content is present.\n- Body sanitization holds in the output.\n- The Navigation bar lists Home, then the Published Pages in nav order.\n- No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears anywhere.\n- Only the Images of Published Recaps are copied.\n\n**Existing CLI entry point: `main(argv)`.** `cms backup` writes a dated archive containing the database and the uploads, and refuses an output folder inside the repository.\n\nThe tests in `tests/test_t00.py` must keep passing.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Comments on the Public site. This is a known gap, and the Instagram footer link stands in for it.\n- Images on Pages, as a possible later addition. Pages are text-only.\n- A hosted or remote admin console. Editors use the Console laptop (ADR-001, ADR-003).\n- Deploy and Backup buttons in the console.\n- Reactivating a Deactivated Account. Admins create a new Account instead (see Further Notes).\n- A live preview, a WYSIWYG editor, drag-and-drop, multi-site, tags and categories, and search.\n- Password reset by email. An Admin sets a new password instead.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n**Coverage of the seven required capabilities**\n\n| # | Capability | Where this spec covers it |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Login: sign in and out, hashed passwords | Stories 1–7. Login, sessions and argon2 decisions. Password and Deactivation tests. |\n| 2 | Two roles with genuinely different permissions | Stories 8–13 and 41. Permission policy. Admin-only route list. Required 403-or-redirect and Recap-ownership tests. |\n| 3 | Posts (here, Recaps and Trips): CRUD, title, Slug, Markdown Body, draft and published, author, timestamps | Stories 14–31. Schema (Owner as author, Byline as the displayed author). Status machine. |\n| 4 | Pages, with published Pages in the nav | Stories 37–41 and 58. Page schema. Navigation bar rendering. |\n| 5 | Admin console: dashboard counts, filterable content list, editor with Preview | Stories 42–49. Console screens. Dashboard, content list and Preview tests. |\n| 6 | User management: Admin-only, create, change role, Deactivate, Deactivated users can't log in | Stories 50–54. Account schema and policy. Deactivation and role tests. |\n| 7 | Public site and publish: only published content, sanitized Markdown, nav from Pages, `cms publish` writes `site/` with relative paths | Stories 55–64. Rendering and sanitizer decisions. Seam 2 tests. |\n\n**Reactivation.** Reactivating a Deactivated Account never came up while the design was settled. This spec leaves it out to keep the Account rules small. Raise it before ticketing if the officers want it.\n\n**Instagram handle.** The footer link is a placeholder, `https://www.instagram.com/REPLACE-ME/`. The owner will replace it with the club's real Instagram handle before the final publish.\n\n**Who can Deploy.** \"Only Admins Deploy and Backup\" is enforced by the Console laptop's terminal being the President's, not by code. This is a deliberate trade-off, made in the spec session.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"09799c01-1495-4ff0-936c-9b90b31cfbbf","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":null,"status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":7},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"andrew-vera26/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"andrew-vera26/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"andrew-vera26/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"macos"}},"sandboxed":false,"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fe2fb1de-28f2-4c58-a78a-ecdbac775830","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"classifier"},"session_id":"9dbdcd61-9843-4f89-8b21-3507a429536c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2","version":"2.1.309","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"ed15f7c6-6046-47ba-b56d-b7e33c89a244","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgMF614RJZR1tGC5VNztG","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've read spec #1, `CONTEXT.md`, the three ADRs, the handoff, and the T00 code and tests. Here is my proposed breakdown. Every ticket is a vertical slice: schema, routes, templates and tests together, each landing green.\n\n## Proposed tickets\n\n**T01: Log in, sign out, CSRF, and the permission policy (tracer bullet)**\n- **Blocked by:** none\n- **Delivers:**\n  - A database with Accounts, and a test fixture that gives each test a temporary database seeded with the demo Admin and Editor.\n  - `/login` and `/logout`, argon2 hashes, and the generic failure message.\n  - Every `/admin` route redirects anonymous visitors to login.\n  - CSRF tokens on the login and logout forms, and a 403 when one is missing.\n  - The one permission-policy module, which rereads the Account on every request.\n  - A table-driven \"Editor on Admin-only routes\" test that starts empty. Each later ticket adds its own rows.\n\n**T02: Account management**\n- **Blocked by:** T01\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Admins list, create, change the role of, and Deactivate Accounts.\n  - A Deactivated Account can't log in, and its live session stops working.\n  - Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused.\n  - Editors get a 403 on every Account route.\n\n**T03: Trips**\n- **Blocked by:** T01\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Admins create, edit, list and delete Trips. The Slug is suggested from the year and city and can be edited.\n  - Editors are refused on every Trip write route.\n\n**T04: Recap Drafts and ownership**\n- **Blocked by:** T03\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Editors create, edit and delete their own Drafts. The Byline is copied from their Account.\n  - Admins enter Recaps for Contributors, typing the Byline, and can edit or delete any Recap.\n  - A Slug that clashes within its Trip is refused, and so is an empty Body.\n  - Owner and timestamps are recorded.\n  - Editors are refused on other people's Recaps.\n  - Deleting a Trip that has Recaps is refused.\n\n**T05: The Recap status machine**\n- **Blocked by:** T04\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Submit, Send back, Publish and Unpublish, with the right person allowed to do each.\n  - Editors are locked out of editing after Submit.\n  - The \"still live until you Deploy\" notice.\n\n**T06: Sanitized Markdown and Preview**\n- **Blocked by:** T04\n- **Delivers:**\n  - The one Body-to-HTML function, which strips scripts and raw HTML, external links and external images.\n  - A Preview button on the Recap editor that shows exactly that output.\n\n**T07: The Public site for Trips and Recaps**\n- **Blocked by:** T05, T06\n- **Delivers:**\n  - `render_site` writes the home page (Trips newest first), the Trip pages (Summary, then Recaps in Published order with their Bylines) and the Recap pages.\n  - Only Published Recaps are written, and only Trips that have one.\n  - Relative paths, computed from each page's depth.\n  - The Instagram footer constant.\n  - The Site state record of when `cms publish` last ran.\n\n**T08: Images**\n- **Blocked by:** T07\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Upload to a Recap: JPEG, PNG or WebP, checked by file content, up to about 5 MB, with required alt text.\n  - A ready-to-paste snippet after each upload.\n  - Publish is refused while an image in the Body has no alt text.\n  - Images are deleted along with their Recap.\n  - `cms publish` copies only the Images of Published Recaps.\n\n**T09: Pages and the Navigation bar**\n- **Blocked by:** T06, T07\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Admins create, edit, delete, Publish and Unpublish Pages, set their nav position, and use Preview. The Pages list filters by status.\n  - Reserved and duplicate Slugs are refused.\n  - The Public site writes Published Pages and a Navigation bar of Home, then Pages in nav order.\n  - Editors are refused on every Page route.\n\n**T10: Dashboard and content list**\n- **Blocked by:** T09\n- **Delivers:**\n  - Admin dashboard counts, and an Editor's counts of their own Recaps.\n  - The \"changes not yet live\" notice.\n  - A Recaps list filtered by status, Trip and Owner, with Submitted shown first for Admins. Editors see only their own.\n\n**T11: `cms backup`**\n- **Blocked by:** T01\n- **Delivers:** a dated zip containing the database and the uploads folder. It refuses any output folder inside the repository, and backup archives are gitignored.\n\n**T12: Full seed data and the final route audit**\n- **Blocked by:** T02, T08, T10, T11\n- **Delivers:**\n  - `seed_demo.py` creates a Trip, Recaps in every status, and a Draft and a Published Page.\n  - A sweep test checks that every console route redirects anonymous visitors and that the Editor table is complete.\n\nThe frontier after T01 is T02, T03 and T11 in parallel. After that, T05 and T06 can run in parallel.\n\n## For you to judge\n\n1. **Granularity.** T04 and T05 are separate so each fits one session. T01 is the largest ticket because CSRF and the policy module have to exist before anything else can be tested. Too coarse, too fine, or about right?\n2. **Blocking edges.**\n   - I made T08 wait for T07 so it can include the copy into `site/`.\n   - T10 waits for T09 because the dashboard counts Pages.\n   - Would you rather T10 come earlier, with the Page counts added later?\n3. **Open questions the handoff says to settle before ticketing.** I'll write whatever you decide into the tickets as decided:\n   - No reactivating Accounts. An Admin creates a new Account instead.\n   - No password reset by email. An Admin sets a new password.\n   - Editors see only their own Recaps, including in the list and on the dashboard.\n   - The Byline is copied onto a Recap when it is created, and renaming an Account later doesn't change it.\n   - The reserved Page Slugs are `trips`, `images`, `admin` and `login`.\n   - The \"Officers and contact\" Page can't link to anything outside the site. Do contact emails need to be allowed, for example as `mailto:` links?\n4. **Labels.** Each issue would get both `ticket` and `ready-for-agent`, with the title prefixed `T0N:` and native GitHub \"blocked by\" links. Is that OK?"}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":8317,"cache_read_input_tokens":64648,"output_tokens":3774,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":1527},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":8317,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":3774,"cache_read_input_tokens":64648,"cache_creation_input_tokens":8317,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":8317},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"b3de701e-5c06-4001-8a5b-d730c717ffe5","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgMF5zkZb538vTmZ1saXS","requestRef":"7a6bc60d-88ab-4777-bc06-32db63eec83e","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","attributionSkill":"to-tickets","type":"assistant","uuid":"891bf726-792c-48c4-bc69-332a12dfb07c","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:47:05.213Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9dbdcd61-9843-4f89-8b21-3507a429536c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2","version":"2.1.309","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"1843e953-b0b1-4791-9756-734d5e056634","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"1de4ffd7-4409-43ff-9a16-105f100363d3","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"\n\n<pasted_content id=\"c288\">\nToo many tickets: the manual says 7 to 9. Merge to 9 and publish those, not the 12. T01 login, sign out, CSRF and the permission policy. T02 Account management. T03 Trips and Recap Drafts with ownership (your old T03 and T04). T04 the Recap status machine, sanitized Markdown and Preview (old T05 and T06). T05 the Public site for Trips and Recaps (old T07). T06 Images (old T08). T07 Pages and the Navigation bar (old T09). T08 Dashboard and content list (old T10). T09 seed data, cms backup and the final route audit (old T11 and T12). Blocking: T02 on T01, T03 on T01, T04 on T03, T05 on T04, T06 on T05, T07 on T04 and T05, T08 on T07, T09 on all. Settled: no reactivating Accounts, no email password reset, Editors see only their own Recaps, Byline copied at creation, reserved Page Slugs are trips, images, admin and login, and contact emails on the Officers Page are plain text with no mailto links. Label each ticket and ready-for-agent, prefix titles with T0N, and give each acceptance criteria and a Blocked by line. Publish them as GitHub issues.\n</pasted_content id=\"c288\">\n"},"uuid":"ab8299cc-956a-43f0-8f7a-7d12c13ac6e6","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:48:44.439Z","permissionMode":"auto","origin":{"kind":"human"},"promptSource":"typed","turnOrigin":"human","turnPosition":{"promptIndex":3,"turnIndex":3},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2","version":"2.1.309","gitBranch":"main"}
