{"parentUuid":"cc51df0e-8311-4a8f-95a0-99adc244ed3e","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgMFG1RGSdCdBBiW9yDLb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Dv3oMpLxiSTqA5tyjjZET2","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"mkdir -p /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-andrewv-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2/scratchpad/tickets && cd /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-andrewv-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2/scratchpad/tickets && \ncat > T01.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nThe tracer bullet through every layer. A person with an Account can log in to the admin console at `/login` with their email and password, and sign out at `/logout`. Anyone who isn't logged in and requests a console page is sent to the login page. Passwords are stored only as argon2 hashes. A failed login shows one generic \"email or password is incorrect\" message. Sessions use a signed cookie. Every form that changes state carries a CSRF token, and a state-changing request without a valid token gets a 403.\n\nThe ticket also builds the single permission-policy module, which answers \"can this Account do this action to this object\". Every route asks it, rather than checking roles itself. Logged out means a redirect to login, and logged in but not allowed means a 403. The policy rereads the Account on every request, so later Deactivations and role changes take effect at once.\n\nTest setup: the `client` and `client_as` fixtures are extended so that each test gets a temporary database seeded with the demo Admin and Editor Accounts, using the emails `client_as` already expects. The seed script creates those same two Accounts, with passwords read from `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`.\n\nThis ticket also starts a table-driven \"Editor on Admin-only routes\" test. Every later ticket adds its own Admin-only routes to that table.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The Account schema has email (unique), name, class year, role (`admin` or `editor`), argon2 password hash, an active flag and a created timestamp.\n- [ ] Logging in with the right email and password works for both seeded Accounts, and `client_as(\"admin\")` and `client_as(\"editor\")` no longer skip.\n- [ ] A wrong password and an unknown email both fail with the same generic message.\n- [ ] The stored password is an argon2 hash and never the plain text. No password is logged.\n- [ ] Signing out ends the session, and console pages redirect to login afterwards.\n- [ ] Anonymous requests to `/admin` routes redirect to `/login`.\n- [ ] Every state-changing form, including login and logout, renders a hidden CSRF token. A POST with a missing or wrong token gets a 403.\n- [ ] Routes ask the one permission-policy module, with no ad-hoc role checks in the routes, and the policy rereads the Account on every request.\n- [ ] The table-driven Editor-on-Admin-only-routes test exists and is ready for later tickets to add rows.\n- [ ] The seed script creates the demo Admin and Editor from environment passwords, and is idempotent on a fresh database.\n- [ ] The tests in `tests/test_t00.py` still pass. They may be updated only where `/admin` now requires login.\n\n## Blocked by\n\nNone (can start immediately)\nEOF\ncat > T02.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nAdmins manage Accounts from the console. They can list Accounts, create one (email, name, class year, role and initial password), change an Account's role between Editor and Admin, and Deactivate an Account. A Deactivated Account can no longer log in, and any session it already has stops working on its next request. Its Recaps, Pages and Bylines stay as they are. Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused, so the club can never lock itself out, and this is what makes Handover safe. Editors are refused on every Account route.\n\nSettled: Accounts cannot be reactivated, because an Admin creates a new Account instead. There is no password reset by email, because an Admin sets a new password.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Admin can list Accounts and create an Account with email, name, class year, role and initial password, which is stored as an argon2 hash.\n- [ ] Creating an Account with an email that is already in use is refused with a clear error.\n- [ ] An Admin can change an Account's role, and the Account's permissions follow the new role on its next request.\n- [ ] An Admin can Deactivate an Account. The Account can't log in afterwards, and its existing session stops working.\n- [ ] Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused, and that Account stays an active Admin.\n- [ ] An Admin can set a new password for an Account.\n- [ ] There is no Reactivate action.\n- [ ] Every Account route is added to the table-driven Editor test, and Editors get a 403 or a redirect on each one.\n- [ ] Every Account form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > T03.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**Trips.** Admins create, edit, list and delete Trips. A Trip has a conference name, host city, start date, end date, Summary and Slug. The Slug is suggested from the start year and city, such as `2026-chicago`, and can be edited. It must be unique among Trips. Deleting a Trip is refused while it still has Recaps. Editors are refused on every Trip write route.\n\n**Recap Drafts with ownership.** An Editor creates a Recap by picking its Trip from a list and entering a title, a Slug (suggested from the title) and a Markdown Body. Every Recap starts as a Draft. The Editor's Byline (name and class year) is copied from their Account when the Recap is created. An Admin entering a Contributor's emailed writing types the Contributor's name and class year as the Byline instead. Each Recap records its Owner and when it was created and last edited. A Recap's Slug must be unique within its Trip. Saving with an empty Body is refused. Editors may view, edit and delete only Drafts they Own. Admins may edit or delete any Recap, and can edit the Byline.\n\nSettled: the Byline is copied at creation, so renaming or Deactivating an Account later doesn't rewrite it. Editors see only their own Recaps.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Admin can create, edit, list and delete Trips. All five fields and the Slug are required.\n- [ ] The Trip Slug is suggested from the year and city, can be edited, and a duplicate is refused with a clear error.\n- [ ] Deleting a Trip that has any Recap is refused, and the Trip remains.\n- [ ] An Editor can create a Recap on an existing Trip, chosen from a list, and the Recap starts as a Draft.\n- [ ] The Recap Slug is suggested from the title. A Slug that clashes within the same Trip is refused with a clear error, and the same Slug on a different Trip is allowed.\n- [ ] Saving a Recap with an empty or whitespace-only Body is refused.\n- [ ] An Editor's Byline is filled in from their Account. An Admin can create a Recap with a typed Contributor Byline, and Owner is still set to the Admin.\n- [ ] Renaming the Owner's Account later doesn't change existing Bylines.\n- [ ] Owner, created and edited timestamps are recorded and shown in the editor.\n- [ ] An Editor can edit and delete their own Draft.\n- [ ] An Editor's requests to view, edit or delete another person's Recap are refused, and that Recap is unchanged.\n- [ ] An Admin can edit or delete any Recap, including its Byline.\n- [ ] The Trip write routes are added to the table-driven Editor test.\n- [ ] Every form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > T04.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**The Recap status machine.** A Recap moves between statuses as follows:\n\n| From | To | Who |\n|---|---|---|\n| Draft | Submitted | the Editor who Owns it |\n| Submitted | Draft (Send back) | Admin |\n| Draft or Submitted | Published | Admin |\n| Published | Draft (Unpublish) | Admin |\n\nThe editor shows only the action buttons the current person may use. Once their Recap is Submitted or Published, an Editor can no longer edit or delete it. Submitting with an empty Body is refused. Publishing records the Published timestamp. When an Admin edits or Unpublishes a Published Recap, a notice says the change is \"still live until you Deploy\".\n\n**Sanitized Markdown and Preview.** One function turns a Body into HTML: `markdown-it-py`, then `nh3` with an allowlist of formatting tags. It strips all raw HTML, any link whose target is not relative, and any image whose source is not one of the Recap's own uploaded Images. Until Images exist, that means every image. `mailto:` links count as external and are removed. The Recap editor gets a Preview button that reloads the page with the Body rendered by exactly this function. Preview and `cms publish` later share it.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Editor can Submit their own Draft, and it becomes Submitted.\n- [ ] An Admin can Send back a Submitted Recap to Draft, Publish a Draft or Submitted Recap, and Unpublish a Published Recap to Draft.\n- [ ] Transitions that aren't in the table are refused, for example Submitting a Published Recap.\n- [ ] An Editor's Publish and Unpublish requests are refused, and the status is unchanged.\n- [ ] An Editor's Submit request on another person's Recap is refused, and the Recap is unchanged.\n- [ ] An Editor can't edit or delete their own Recap once it is Submitted or Published.\n- [ ] Submitting a Recap with an empty Body is refused.\n- [ ] Publishing sets the Published timestamp.\n- [ ] Editing or Unpublishing a Published Recap shows the \"still live until you Deploy\" notice.\n- [ ] Preview, given a Body containing `<script>`, an external link and an external image, shows sanitized HTML with all three removed and the ordinary Markdown formatting intact.\n- [ ] A `mailto:` link in a Body is removed, and its text remains as plain text.\n- [ ] Publish, Unpublish and Send back are added to the table-driven Editor test.\n- [ ] Every action form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #4 (T03)\nEOF\ncat > T05.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n`cms publish` renders the Public site for Trips and Recaps into the output folder. The home page lists Trips that have at least one Published Recap, newest start date first. Each such Trip gets a page at `trips/<trip>/` showing its Summary and then its Published Recaps in the order they were Published, with their Bylines. Each Published Recap gets a page at `trips/<trip>/<recap>/`. Every address is an index file. Every link and stylesheet reference is relative, computed from the page's depth. Bodies go through the shared sanitizer from T04. A footer links to the club's Instagram for discussion. The link is a constant committed in settings, with the placeholder `https://www.instagram.com/REPLACE-ME/`, and not an environment variable. The renderer never reads content that isn't Published. Each run records when `cms publish` last ran in the Site state record, which T08 uses for its \"changes not yet live\" notice.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `render_site(out)` writes the home page, one page per Trip that has a Published Recap, and one page per Published Recap, at the addresses above.\n- [ ] Draft and Submitted Recaps are absent from the output, and so are Trips with no Published Recap.\n- [ ] The home page orders Trips by newest start date first.\n- [ ] A Trip page shows its Summary, then its Published Recaps in Published order, each with its Byline.\n- [ ] A Body containing `<script>`, an external link and an external image is sanitized in the output.\n- [ ] No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears in any file in the output, and links from nested pages resolve correctly.\n- [ ] Every page has the Instagram footer link, taken from the committed constant.\n- [ ] Running `cms publish` updates the Site state record of when it last ran.\n- [ ] The tests in `tests/test_t00.py` still pass.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #5 (T04)\nEOF\ncat > T06.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nAn Editor, or an Admin, uploads photos to a Recap they are allowed to edit. The accepted types are JPEG, PNG and WebP, checked by file content and not by extension, up to about 5 MB. Alt text is required. Uploads are stored in a gitignored uploads folder outside the output folder. After an upload, the console shows a ready-to-paste Markdown snippet with the alt text filled in and a relative `images/<file>` reference. The sanitizer allows only images that point to the Recap's own uploaded Images. Publish is refused while the Body has an image without alt text. Deleting a Recap deletes its Images. `cms publish` copies a Published Recap's Images next to its page so the relative reference resolves, and copies no Images from Recaps that aren't Published.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Uploading a valid JPEG, PNG or WebP with alt text to a Recap succeeds and shows a Markdown snippet with that alt text and a relative `images/<file>` path.\n- [ ] A file of the wrong type is refused with a clear message. This includes a non-image renamed to `.jpg`.\n- [ ] A file over the size limit is refused with a clear message.\n- [ ] An upload with missing alt text is refused.\n- [ ] An Editor can't upload to another person's Recap, or to their own Recap once it is Submitted or Published.\n- [ ] Publish is refused while the Body contains an image with empty alt text.\n- [ ] Preview shows the Recap's own uploaded Images, and still strips external images.\n- [ ] Deleting a Recap removes its Images from storage.\n- [ ] `render_site` copies only the Images of Published Recaps, and an Image from a Draft Recap is absent from the output.\n- [ ] Image references in the output are relative and resolve.\n- [ ] The upload form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #6 (T05)\nEOF\ncat > T07.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nAdmins create, edit, delete, Publish and Unpublish Pages. A Page has a title, a Slug, a text-only Markdown Body, a Draft or Published status and a nav position. Each Page records its Owner and when it was created and last edited. The Page editor has a Preview that uses the shared sanitizer. The Pages list can be filtered by status. A Page Slug is refused if it is reserved (`trips`, `images`, `admin` or `login`) or already used by another Page. Editing or Unpublishing a Published Page shows the \"still live until you Deploy\" notice. Editors are refused on every Page route.\n\nOn the Public site, each Published Page is written at `<page>/`. Every page carries a Navigation bar of Home first, then the Published Pages ordered by nav position.\n\nSettled: Pages are text-only and have no Images. Contact emails on an \"Officers and contact\" Page are plain text, and `mailto:` links are stripped like any other external link.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Admin can create, edit, delete, Publish and Unpublish a Page. Every Page starts as a Draft.\n- [ ] Owner, created and edited timestamps are recorded.\n- [ ] A reserved Slug (`trips`, `images`, `admin`, `login`) and a duplicate Page Slug are each refused with a clear error.\n- [ ] The Pages list filters by status.\n- [ ] Page Preview shows the sanitized Body, with external links, `mailto:` links, images and raw HTML removed.\n- [ ] Editing or Unpublishing a Published Page shows the \"still live until you Deploy\" notice.\n- [ ] Every Page route is added to the table-driven Editor test, and Editors get a 403 or a redirect on each one.\n- [ ] `render_site` writes Published Pages and omits Draft Pages.\n- [ ] Every page in the output has a Navigation bar listing Home, then the Published Pages in nav order, with relative links that resolve from every depth.\n- [ ] No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears anywhere in the output.\n- [ ] Every Page form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #5 (T04)\n- #6 (T05)\nEOF\ncat > T08.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**Dashboard.** An Admin's dashboard shows counts of Recaps by status (Draft, Submitted, Published), Trips, Pages by status, and active Accounts. An Editor's dashboard shows counts of their own Recaps by status only. An Admin sees a \"changes not yet live\" notice whenever any Trip, Recap or Page has changed since the last `cms publish`, as recorded in the Site state record.\n\n**Content list.** An Admin's Recaps list can be filtered by status, Trip and Owner, and shows Submitted Recaps first. An Editor's list shows only their own Recaps, filterable by status and Trip, with no Owner filter.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The Admin dashboard counts match the test data: Recaps by status, Trips, Pages by status, and active Accounts, with Deactivated Accounts excluded.\n- [ ] The Editor dashboard shows only counts of that Editor's own Recaps.\n- [ ] The \"changes not yet live\" notice appears after a content change, disappears after `cms publish`, and appears again after the next change.\n- [ ] The Admin Recaps list filters correctly by status, Trip and Owner, alone and combined.\n- [ ] Submitted Recaps appear first in the Admin list.\n- [ ] An Editor's list contains only their own Recaps, the status and Trip filters work, and an Owner filter can't reveal anyone else's Recaps.\n- [ ] Anonymous requests to the dashboard and list redirect to login.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #8 (T07)\nEOF\ncat > T09.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**Seed data.** The seed script runs clean on a fresh clone with the `.env.example` values. It creates the demo Admin and Editor, with emails matching the test fixture and passwords from `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`. It also creates a Trip, Recaps in each status (Draft, Submitted, Published), and one Draft and one Published Page, so a grader can try every capability straight away.\n\n**`cms backup`.** A new CLI subcommand. It takes an online-backup copy of the SQLite database and the uploads folder, and zips them with the standard library into `cms-backup-<YYYYMMDD-HHMM>.zip` in an output folder given as an argument. It refuses any output folder inside the repository. Backup archives are also gitignored as a second safeguard.\n\n**Final route audit.** A sweep confirms that every console route redirects anonymous visitors to login, and that the table-driven Editor test covers every Admin-only route and action listed in the spec.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The seed script on a fresh database creates the Admin, the Editor, a Trip, at least one Recap in each status, a Draft Page and a Published Page, and exits 0.\n- [ ] The seed script exits non-zero with a clear message when either password variable is missing. No password is hard-coded.\n- [ ] Running `cms publish` after seeding produces a site with the Published content only.\n- [ ] `main([\"backup\", <folder>])` writes one dated archive containing the database and the uploaded Images.\n- [ ] `cms backup` with an output folder inside the repository is refused with a non-zero exit, and no archive is written.\n- [ ] `.gitignore` excludes backup /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-andrewv-code-iphs400-mp2-cms/15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2/scratchpad/tickets && \ncat > T01.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nThe tracer bullet through every layer. A person with an Account can log in to the admin console at `/login` with their email and password, and sign out at `/logout`. Anyone who isn't logged in and requests a console page is sent to the login page. Passwords are stored only as argon2 hashes. A failed login shows one generic \"email or password is incorrect\" message. Sessions use a signed cookie. Every form that changes state carries a CSRF token, and a state-changing request without a valid token gets a 403.\n\nThe ticket also builds the single permission-policy module, which answers \"can this Account do this action to this object\". Every route asks it, rather than checking roles itself. Logged out means a redirect to login, and logged in but not allowed means a 403. The policy rereads the Account on every request, so later Deactivations and role changes take effect at once.\n\nTest setup: the `client` and `client_as` fixtures are extended so that each test gets a temporary database seeded with the demo Admin and Editor Accounts, using the emails `client_as` already expects. The seed script creates those same two Accounts, with passwords read from `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`.\n\nThis ticket also starts a table-driven \"Editor on Admin-only routes\" test. Every later ticket adds its own Admin-only routes to that table.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The Account schema has email (unique), name, class year, role (`admin` or `editor`), argon2 password hash, an active flag and a created timestamp.\n- [ ] Logging in with the right email and password works for both seeded Accounts, and `client_as(\"admin\")` and `client_as(\"editor\")` no longer skip.\n- [ ] A wrong password and an unknown email both fail with the same generic message.\n- [ ] The stored password is an argon2 hash and never the plain text. No password is logged.\n- [ ] Signing out ends the session, and console pages redirect to login afterwards.\n- [ ] Anonymous requests to `/admin` routes redirect to `/login`.\n- [ ] Every state-changing form, including login and logout, renders a hidden CSRF token. A POST with a missing or wrong token gets a 403.\n- [ ] Routes ask the one permission-policy module, with no ad-hoc role checks in the routes, and the policy rereads the Account on every request.\n- [ ] The table-driven Editor-on-Admin-only-routes test exists and is ready for later tickets to add rows.\n- [ ] The seed script creates the demo Admin and Editor from environment passwords, and is idempotent on a fresh database.\n- [ ] The tests in `tests/test_t00.py` still pass. They may be updated only where `/admin` now requires login.\n\n## Blocked by\n\nNone (can start immediately)\nEOF\ncat > T02.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nAdmins manage Accounts from the console. They can list Accounts, create one (email, name, class year, role and initial password), change an Account's role between Editor and Admin, and Deactivate an Account. A Deactivated Account can no longer log in, and any session it already has stops working on its next request. Its Recaps, Pages and Bylines stay as they are. Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused, so the club can never lock itself out, and this is what makes Handover safe. Editors are refused on every Account route.\n\nSettled: Accounts cannot be reactivated, because an Admin creates a new Account instead. There is no password reset by email, because an Admin sets a new password.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Admin can list Accounts and create an Account with email, name, class year, role and initial password, which is stored as an argon2 hash.\n- [ ] Creating an Account with an email that is already in use is refused with a clear error.\n- [ ] An Admin can change an Account's role, and the Account's permissions follow the new role on its next request.\n- [ ] An Admin can Deactivate an Account. The Account can't log in afterwards, and its existing session stops working.\n- [ ] Deactivating or demoting the last active Admin is refused, and that Account stays an active Admin.\n- [ ] An Admin can set a new password for an Account.\n- [ ] There is no Reactivate action.\n- [ ] Every Account route is added to the table-driven Editor test, and Editors get a 403 or a redirect on each one.\n- [ ] Every Account form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > T03.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**Trips.** Admins create, edit, list and delete Trips. A Trip has a conference name, host city, start date, end date, Summary and Slug. The Slug is suggested from the start year and city, such as `2026-chicago`, and can be edited. It must be unique among Trips. Deleting a Trip is refused while it still has Recaps. Editors are refused on every Trip write route.\n\n**Recap Drafts with ownership.** An Editor creates a Recap by picking its Trip from a list and entering a title, a Slug (suggested from the title) and a Markdown Body. Every Recap starts as a Draft. The Editor's Byline (name and class year) is copied from their Account when the Recap is created. An Admin entering a Contributor's emailed writing types the Contributor's name and class year as the Byline instead. Each Recap records its Owner and when it was created and last edited. A Recap's Slug must be unique within its Trip. Saving with an empty Body is refused. Editors may view, edit and delete only Drafts they Own. Admins may edit or delete any Recap, and can edit the Byline.\n\nSettled: the Byline is copied at creation, so renaming or Deactivating an Account later doesn't rewrite it. Editors see only their own Recaps.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Admin can create, edit, list and delete Trips. All five fields and the Slug are required.\n- [ ] The Trip Slug is suggested from the year and city, can be edited, and a duplicate is refused with a clear error.\n- [ ] Deleting a Trip that has any Recap is refused, and the Trip remains.\n- [ ] An Editor can create a Recap on an existing Trip, chosen from a list, and the Recap starts as a Draft.\n- [ ] The Recap Slug is suggested from the title. A Slug that clashes within the same Trip is refused with a clear error, and the same Slug on a different Trip is allowed.\n- [ ] Saving a Recap with an empty or whitespace-only Body is refused.\n- [ ] An Editor's Byline is filled in from their Account. An Admin can create a Recap with a typed Contributor Byline, and Owner is still set to the Admin.\n- [ ] Renaming the Owner's Account later doesn't change existing Bylines.\n- [ ] Owner, created and edited timestamps are recorded and shown in the editor.\n- [ ] An Editor can edit and delete their own Draft.\n- [ ] An Editor's requests to view, edit or delete another person's Recap are refused, and that Recap is unchanged.\n- [ ] An Admin can edit or delete any Recap, including its Byline.\n- [ ] The Trip write routes are added to the table-driven Editor test.\n- [ ] Every form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\nEOF\ncat > T04.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**The Recap status machine.** A Recap moves between statuses as follows:\n\n| From | To | Who |\n|---|---|---|\n| Draft | Submitted | the Editor who Owns it |\n| Submitted | Draft (Send back) | Admin |\n| Draft or Submitted | Published | Admin |\n| Published | Draft (Unpublish) | Admin |\n\nThe editor shows only the action buttons the current person may use. Once their Recap is Submitted or Published, an Editor can no longer edit or delete it. Submitting with an empty Body is refused. Publishing records the Published timestamp. When an Admin edits or Unpublishes a Published Recap, a notice says the change is \"still live until you Deploy\".\n\n**Sanitized Markdown and Preview.** One function turns a Body into HTML: `markdown-it-py`, then `nh3` with an allowlist of formatting tags. It strips all raw HTML, any link whose target is not relative, and any image whose source is not one of the Recap's own uploaded Images. Until Images exist, that means every image. `mailto:` links count as external and are removed. The Recap editor gets a Preview button that reloads the page with the Body rendered by exactly this function. Preview and `cms publish` later share it.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Editor can Submit their own Draft, and it becomes Submitted.\n- [ ] An Admin can Send back a Submitted Recap to Draft, Publish a Draft or Submitted Recap, and Unpublish a Published Recap to Draft.\n- [ ] Transitions that aren't in the table are refused, for example Submitting a Published Recap.\n- [ ] An Editor's Publish and Unpublish requests are refused, and the status is unchanged.\n- [ ] An Editor's Submit request on another person's Recap is refused, and the Recap is unchanged.\n- [ ] An Editor can't edit or delete their own Recap once it is Submitted or Published.\n- [ ] Submitting a Recap with an empty Body is refused.\n- [ ] Publishing sets the Published timestamp.\n- [ ] Editing or Unpublishing a Published Recap shows the \"still live until you Deploy\" notice.\n- [ ] Preview, given a Body containing `<script>`, an external link and an external image, shows sanitized HTML with all three removed and the ordinary Markdown formatting intact.\n- [ ] A `mailto:` link in a Body is removed, and its text remains as plain text.\n- [ ] Publish, Unpublish and Send back are added to the table-driven Editor test.\n- [ ] Every action form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #4 (T03)\nEOF\ncat > T05.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n`cms publish` renders the Public site for Trips and Recaps into the output folder. The home page lists Trips that have at least one Published Recap, newest start date first. Each such Trip gets a page at `trips/<trip>/` showing its Summary and then its Published Recaps in the order they were Published, with their Bylines. Each Published Recap gets a page at `trips/<trip>/<recap>/`. Every address is an index file. Every link and stylesheet reference is relative, computed from the page's depth. Bodies go through the shared sanitizer from T04. A footer links to the club's Instagram for discussion. The link is a constant committed in settings, with the placeholder `https://www.instagram.com/REPLACE-ME/`, and not an environment variable. The renderer never reads content that isn't Published. Each run records when `cms publish` last ran in the Site state record, which T08 uses for its \"changes not yet live\" notice.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `render_site(out)` writes the home page, one page per Trip that has a Published Recap, and one page per Published Recap, at the addresses above.\n- [ ] Draft and Submitted Recaps are absent from the output, and so are Trips with no Published Recap.\n- [ ] The home page orders Trips by newest start date first.\n- [ ] A Trip page shows its Summary, then its Published Recaps in Published order, each with its Byline.\n- [ ] A Body containing `<script>`, an external link and an external image is sanitized in the output.\n- [ ] No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears in any file in the output, and links from nested pages resolve correctly.\n- [ ] Every page has the Instagram footer link, taken from the committed constant.\n- [ ] Running `cms publish` updates the Site state record of when it last ran.\n- [ ] The tests in `tests/test_t00.py` still pass.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #5 (T04)\nEOF\ncat > T06.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nAn Editor, or an Admin, uploads photos to a Recap they are allowed to edit. The accepted types are JPEG, PNG and WebP, checked by file content and not by extension, up to about 5 MB. Alt text is required. Uploads are stored in a gitignored uploads folder outside the output folder. After an upload, the console shows a ready-to-paste Markdown snippet with the alt text filled in and a relative `images/<file>` reference. The sanitizer allows only images that point to the Recap's own uploaded Images. Publish is refused while the Body has an image without alt text. Deleting a Recap deletes its Images. `cms publish` copies a Published Recap's Images next to its page so the relative reference resolves, and copies no Images from Recaps that aren't Published.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Uploading a valid JPEG, PNG or WebP with alt text to a Recap succeeds and shows a Markdown snippet with that alt text and a relative `images/<file>` path.\n- [ ] A file of the wrong type is refused with a clear message. This includes a non-image renamed to `.jpg`.\n- [ ] A file over the size limit is refused with a clear message.\n- [ ] An upload with missing alt text is refused.\n- [ ] An Editor can't upload to another person's Recap, or to their own Recap once it is Submitted or Published.\n- [ ] Publish is refused while the Body contains an image with empty alt text.\n- [ ] Preview shows the Recap's own uploaded Images, and still strips external images.\n- [ ] Deleting a Recap removes its Images from storage.\n- [ ] `render_site` copies only the Images of Published Recaps, and an Image from a Draft Recap is absent from the output.\n- [ ] Image references in the output are relative and resolve.\n- [ ] The upload form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #6 (T05)\nEOF\ncat > T07.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\nAdmins create, edit, delete, Publish and Unpublish Pages. A Page has a title, a Slug, a text-only Markdown Body, a Draft or Published status and a nav position. Each Page records its Owner and when it was created and last edited. The Page editor has a Preview that uses the shared sanitizer. The Pages list can be filtered by status. A Page Slug is refused if it is reserved (`trips`, `images`, `admin` or `login`) or already used by another Page. Editing or Unpublishing a Published Page shows the \"still live until you Deploy\" notice. Editors are refused on every Page route.\n\nOn the Public site, each Published Page is written at `<page>/`. Every page carries a Navigation bar of Home first, then the Published Pages ordered by nav position.\n\nSettled: Pages are text-only and have no Images. Contact emails on an \"Officers and contact\" Page are plain text, and `mailto:` links are stripped like any other external link.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] An Admin can create, edit, delete, Publish and Unpublish a Page. Every Page starts as a Draft.\n- [ ] Owner, created and edited timestamps are recorded.\n- [ ] A reserved Slug (`trips`, `images`, `admin`, `login`) and a duplicate Page Slug are each refused with a clear error.\n- [ ] The Pages list filters by status.\n- [ ] Page Preview shows the sanitized Body, with external links, `mailto:` links, images and raw HTML removed.\n- [ ] Editing or Unpublishing a Published Page shows the \"still live until you Deploy\" notice.\n- [ ] Every Page route is added to the table-driven Editor test, and Editors get a 403 or a redirect on each one.\n- [ ] `render_site` writes Published Pages and omits Draft Pages.\n- [ ] Every page in the output has a Navigation bar listing Home, then the Published Pages in nav order, with relative links that resolve from every depth.\n- [ ] No `href=\"/` or `src=\"/` appears anywhere in the output.\n- [ ] Every Page form carries a CSRF token.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #5 (T04)\n- #6 (T05)\nEOF\ncat > T08.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**Dashboard.** An Admin's dashboard shows counts of Recaps by status (Draft, Submitted, Published), Trips, Pages by status, and active Accounts. An Editor's dashboard shows counts of their own Recaps by status only. An Admin sees a \"changes not yet live\" notice whenever any Trip, Recap or Page has changed since the last `cms publish`, as recorded in the Site state record.\n\n**Content list.** An Admin's Recaps list can be filtered by status, Trip and Owner, and shows Submitted Recaps first. An Editor's list shows only their own Recaps, filterable by status and Trip, with no Owner filter.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The Admin dashboard counts match the test data: Recaps by status, Trips, Pages by status, and active Accounts, with Deactivated Accounts excluded.\n- [ ] The Editor dashboard shows only counts of that Editor's own Recaps.\n- [ ] The \"changes not yet live\" notice appears after a content change, disappears after `cms publish`, and appears again after the next change.\n- [ ] The Admin Recaps list filters correctly by status, Trip and Owner, alone and combined.\n- [ ] Submitted Recaps appear first in the Admin list.\n- [ ] An Editor's list contains only their own Recaps, the status and Trip filters work, and an Owner filter can't reveal anyone else's Recaps.\n- [ ] Anonymous requests to the dashboard and list redirect to login.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #8 (T07)\nEOF\ncat > T09.md <<'EOF'\n## Parent\n\n#1 (Spec: Kenyon Model UN Recaps CMS)\n\n## What to build\n\n**Seed data.** The seed script runs clean on a fresh clone with the `.env.example` values. It creates the demo Admin and Editor, with emails matching the test fixture and passwords from `CMS_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `CMS_EDITOR_PASSWORD`. It also creates a Trip, Recaps in each status (Draft, Submitted, Published), and one Draft and one Published Page, so a grader can try every capability straight away.\n\n**`cms backup`.** A new CLI subcommand. It takes an online-backup copy of the SQLite database and the uploads folder, and zips them with the standard library into `cms-backup-<YYYYMMDD-HHMM>.zip` in an output folder given as an argument. It refuses any output folder inside the repository. Backup archives are also gitignored as a second safeguard.\n\n**Final route audit.** A sweep confirms that every console route redirects anonymous visitors to login, and that the table-driven Editor test covers every Admin-only route and action listed in the spec.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] The seed script on a fresh database creates the Admin, the Editor, a Trip, at least one Recap in each status, a Draft Page and a Published Page, and exits 0.\n- [ ] The seed script exits non-zero with a clear message when either password variable is missing. No password is hard-coded.\n- [ ] Running `cms publish` after seeding produces a site with the Published content only.\n- [ ] `main([\"backup\", <folder>])` writes one dated archive containing the database and the uploaded Images.\n- [ ] `cms backup` with an output folder inside the repository is refused with a non-zero exit, and no archive is written.\n- [ ] `.gitignore` excludes backup archives.\n- [ ] A test requests every console route anonymously and gets a redirect to login for each one.\n- [ ] The Editor table covers Trip writes, all Page routes, all Account routes, Publish, Unpublish, Send back, and edit, Submit and delete on a Recap the Editor doesn't Own.\n- [ ] The full test suite passes, including `tests/test_t00.py`.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)\n- #3 (T02)\n- #4 (T03)\n- #5 (T04)\n- #6 (T05)\n- #7 (T06)\n- #8 (T07)\n- #9 (T08)\nEOF\nls","description":"Write nine ticket bodies to scratchpad"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"2bac9e40-c580-4eee-a6e6-4a9e5517eeb5","apiBlockIndex":1,"requestId":"req_011CgMFG17AiKgNHqqxjEmxC","requestRef":"b1d98091-484c-4561-895a-9210455bcb8b","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","type":"assistant","uuid":"7116b7c4-74d5-4be8-a47e-af72a902a9c9","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:49:46.514Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9dbdcd61-9843-4f89-8b21-3507a429536c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2","version":"2.1.309","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"e5f8d37e-548e-4ab9-822b-a9967b225b45","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CgMFQX3DN5kM6Lpsvv8dB","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've published the nine tickets as GitHub issues #2 to #10. Each has the `ticket` and `ready-for-agent` labels, a `T0N:` title, acceptance criteria, a \"Parent: #1\" section and a \"Blocked by\" line. I also added GitHub's own \"blocked by\" links, matching the edges you listed, and checked them through the API.\n\n| Issue | Ticket | Blocked by |\n|---|---|---|\n| #2 | T01: Log in, sign out, CSRF and the permission policy | none |\n| #3 | T02: Account management | #2 |\n| #4 | T03: Trips and Recap Drafts with ownership | #2 |\n| #5 | T04: Recap status machine, sanitized Markdown and Preview | #4 |\n| #6 | T05: Public site for Trips and Recaps | #5 |\n| #7 | T06: Images | #6 |\n| #8 | T07: Pages and the Navigation bar | #5, #6 |\n| #9 | T08: Dashboard and content list | #8 |\n| #10 | T09: Seed data, cms backup and the final route audit | #2 to #9 |\n\nWhere your settled decisions went:\n- **No reactivating Accounts, and no email password reset:** criteria in T02. T02 also includes an Admin setting a new password for an Account.\n- **Byline copied at creation, and Editors seeing only their own Recaps:** criteria in T03. T08 also limits an Editor's dashboard and list to their own Recaps.\n- **Reserved Page Slugs:** a criterion in T07.\n- **Plain-text contact emails:** handled by the sanitizer in T04, which removes `mailto:` links and keeps their text. T07 tests this on Pages.\n\nI left spec #1 unchanged and didn't make the tickets sub-issues of it, because that would have modified the parent. The frontier now is T01 (#2).\n\nT01's body says the `tests/test_t00.py` tests may be updated where `/admin` now needs a login. The current admin test expects a 200 from an anonymous visitor, which T01 has to change.\n\nWhen you start T01, write `T01` into `.claude/state/phase`."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":975,"cache_read_input_tokens":85936,"output_tokens":716,"output_tokens_details":{"thinking_tokens":0},"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":975,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"global","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":716,"cache_read_input_tokens":85936,"cache_creation_input_tokens":975,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":975},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard","fallback_credit":null},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"serverClassifierRequest":"92c1829c-a8b0-42f3-9636-4c8ffb42125b","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CgMFQWe9jowFHGhNCXkVa","requestRef":"a0470e79-ac63-439c-8215-1d2e84fdb3f1","requestedModel":"claude-opus-5-5","type":"assistant","uuid":"d8078170-bdd1-456c-8b15-e229838d8f42","timestamp":"2026-10-24T14:50:47.960Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"9dbdcd61-9843-4f89-8b21-3507a429536c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/andrewv/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"15c67fdb-ccab-4293-be9a-a1c928dffdb2","version":"2.1.309","gitBranch":"main"}
